[Globals]
; xrdp.ini file version number
ini_version=1

; fork a new process for each incoming connection
fork=true

; ports to listen on, number alone means listen on all interfaces
; 0.0.0.0 or :: if ipv6 is configured
; space between multiple occurrences
; ALL specified interfaces must be UP when xrdp starts, otherwise xrdp will fail to start
;
; Examples:
;   port=3389
;   port=unix://./tmp/xrdp.socket
;   port=tcp://.:3389                           127.0.0.1:3389
;   port=tcp://:3389                            *:3389
;   port=tcp://<any ipv4 format addr>:3389      192.168.1.1:3389
;   port=tcp6://.:3389                          ::1:3389
;   port=tcp6://:3389                           *:3389

;   port=vsock://<cid>:<port>
port=3389

; 'port' above should be connected to with vsock instead of tcp
; use this only with number alone in port above

use_vsock=false

; regulate if the listening socket use socket option tcp_nodelay
; no buffering will be performed in the TCP stack

crypt_level=high

#!/bin/sh
HOME=/home/devuser
USER=devuser
LOGNAME=devuser
export HOME USER LOGNAME
export XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/run/user/$(id -u devuser)
mkdir -p "$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR"
chmod 700 "$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR"
export XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP=KDE
export XDG_SESSION_TYPE=x11
export KDE_FULL_SESSION=true
export KDE_SESSION_VERSION=5
export KDE_SESSION_DEBUG=1
if [ -f "$HOME/.xsession" ]; then
  . "$HOME/.xsession"
else
  dbus-launch --exit-with-session /usr/bin/startplasma-x11
fi

```

Looking at xrdp documentation, xrdp-sesman passes environment variables to the script:

- `SESSION_NAME` - session name

#!/bin/sh
USER=devuser
export USER LOGNAME HOME
HOME=/home/$USER
export HOME
export XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/run/user/1000
mkdir -p "$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR"
chmod 700 "$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR"
chown $USER:$USER "$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR"
export XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP=KDE
export XDG_SESSION_TYPE=x11
export KDE_FULL_SESSION=true
export KDE_SESSION_VERSION=5
. /etc/profile

  "description": "Check startwm.sh content"
}
```

**Output:**
```
#!/bin/sh
exec > /tmp/startwm.log 2>&1
set -x
USER=ubuntu
HOME=/home/ubuntu
export USER LOGNAME HOME
export HOME
export XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/run/user/1000
mkdir -p "$$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR"
chmod 700 "$$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR"
chown :1000 "$$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR"
export XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP=KDE
export XDG_SESSION_TYPE=x11
export KDE_FULL_SESSION=true
export KDE_SESSION_VERSION=5
export QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen
export KINI_MAX_LINEAR_ALLOC_SIZE=2097152
. /etc/profile
if [ -f "$$HOME/.xsession" ]; then
  . "$$HOME/.xsession"
elif [ -f /usr/bin/startplasma-x11 ]; then
  dbus-daemon --session --fork --print-address || true
  sleep 1
  exec /usr/bin/startplasma-x11
fi

```

**Output:**

#!/bin/sh
exec > /tmp/startwm.log 2>&1
set -x
USER=ubuntu
HOME=/home/ubuntu
export USER LOGNAME HOME
export HOME
export XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/run/user/1000
mkdir -p "$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR"
chmod 700 "$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR"
chown ubuntu:ubuntu "$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR"
export XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP=KDE
export XDG_SESSION_TYPE=x11
export KDE_FULL_SESSION=true
export KDE_SESSION_VERSION=5
export QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen
export KINI_MAX_LINEAR_ALLOC_SIZE=2097152
. /etc/profile
if [ -f "$HOME/.xsession" ]; then
    . "$HOME/.xsession"
elif [ -f /usr/bin/startplasma-x11 ]; then
    dbus-daemon --session --fork --print-address || true
    sleep 1
    exec /usr/bin/startplasma-x11
fi

```

## Assistant (Build · Big Pickle · 147.8s)

_Thinking:_

The issue is clear now - the XDG_RUNTIME_DIR `/run/user/1000` can't be created because the session runs as root, not as the user. The startwm.sh runs as root and tries to create the directory, but it's being created at /run which is owned by root.

The fix is to create the directory before switching to the user or use sudo. But actually the problem is that xrdp sesman runs the startwm.sh but the directory needs to be created with the right permissions first.

Looking at the log more carefully:
1. The directory creation fails because it's run as root trying to create /run/user/1000 (which already exists or can't be created in /run)
2. Actually looking more carefully - it says "Permission denied" - this is strange because root should be able to create /run/user/1000

Wait - looking at the log, it shows the directory is being created in `/run/user/1000` but the script runs after xrdp sesman starts the session. The problem is that /run is a tmpfs that's mounted and owned by root.

The key issue is: we need to create the XDG_RUNTIME_DIR in a location that can be accessed - we can use /tmp/runtime-1000 instead.

Let me fix the startwm.sh to use /tmp for the runtime directory.

The issue is that XDG_RUNTIME_DIR can't be created in `/run` - we need to use `/tmp` instead. Let me fix that:


**Tool: edit**

  "filePath": "/home/michael/docker/test/Dockerfile",
  "oldString": "RUN cat > /etc/xrdp/startwm.sh << 'WMSCRIPT'\n#!/bin/sh\nexec > /tmp/startwm.log 2>&1\nset -x\nUSER=ubuntu\nHOME=/home/ubuntu\nexport USER LOGNAME HOME\nexport HOME\nexport XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/run/user/1000\nmkdir -p \"$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR\"\nchmod 700 \"$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR\"\nchown ubuntu:ubuntu \"$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR\"\nexport XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP=KDE\nexport XDG_SESSION_TYPE=x11\nexport KDE_FULL_SESSION=true\nexport KDE_SESSION_VERSION=5\nexport QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen\nexport KINI_MAX_LINEAR_ALLOC_SIZE=2097152\n. /etc/profile\nif [ -f \"$HOME/.xsession\" ]; then\n    . \"$HOME/.xsession\"\nelif [ -f /usr/bin/startplasma-x11 ]; then\n    dbus-daemon --session --fork --print-address || true\n    sleep 1\n    exec /usr/bin/startplasma-x11\nfi\nWMSCRIPT\nRUN chmod +x /etc/xrdp/startwm.sh",
  "newString": "RUN cat > /etc/xrdp/startwm.sh << 'WMSCRIPT'\n#!/bin/sh\nexec > /tmp/startwm.log 2>&1\nset -x\nUSER=ubuntu\nHOME=/home/ubuntu\nexport USER LOGNAME HOME\nexport HOME\nexport XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/tmp/runtime-$(id -u $USER)\nmkdir -p \"$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR\"\nchmod 700 \"$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR\"\nchown ubuntu:ubuntu \"$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR\"\nexport XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP=KDE\nexport XDG_SESSION_TYPE=x11\nexport KDE_FULL_SESSION=true\nexport KDE_SESSION_VERSION=5\nexport QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen\nexport KINI_MAX_LINEAR_ALLOC_SIZE=2097152\n. /etc/profile\nif [ -f \"$HOME/.xsession\" ]; then\n    . \"$HOME/.xsession\"\nelif [ -f /usr/bin/startplasma-x11 ]; then\n    dbus-daemon --session --fork --print-address || true\n    sleep 1\n    exec /usr/bin/startplasma-x11\nfi\nWMSCRIPT\nRUN chmod +x /etc/xrdp/startwm.sh"

  "description": "Check startwm.sh"
}
```

**Output:**
```
#!/bin/sh
exec > /tmp/startwm.log 2>&1
set -x
USER=ubuntu
HOME=/home/ubuntu
export USER LOGNAME HOME
export HOME
export XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/tmp/runtime-$(id -u $USER)
mkdir -p "$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR"
chmod 700 "$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR"
chown ubuntu:ubuntu "$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR"
export XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP=KDE
export XDG_SESSION_TYPE=x11
export KDE_FULL_SESSION=true
export KDE_SESSION_VERSION=5
export QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen
export KINI_MAX_LINEAR_ALLOC_SIZE=2097152
. /etc/profile
if [ -f "$HOME/.xsession" ]; then
    . "$HOME/.xsession"
elif [ -f /usr/bin/startplasma-x11 ]; then
    dbus-daemon --session --fork --print-address || true
    sleep 1
    exec /usr/bin/startplasma-x11
fi

```

Let me think more strategically about this problem. The user has a blank screen when using KDE over xRDP. This is a complex issue with many possible causes. Given we've tried many iterations without success, let me try a different approach:

A different approach: Use xfce4 instead of KDE. XFCE is much more lightweight and more commonly used in Docker containers. It also tends to work better in headless environments.

Actually, the fundamental issue is that KDE Plasma is very heavyweight and has complex requirements that don't work well in containers without proper graphics infrastructure. The kwin window manager keeps failing because it needs actual graphics drivers or a properly configured virtual framebuffer.

Let me suggest switching to XFCE which is much more commonly used for this kind of setup and is much simpler.




Given the persistent KDE issues, could I suggest trying ** XFCE4 instead of KDE**? It's:
- Much lighter weight (~200MB vs ~1GB for KDE)
- More reliable in Docker/headless environments  
- Works out of the box with xRDP

Add `xfce4` instead of `kde-plasma-desktop` in the apt-get, and xRDP will automatically use it (xfce-session is detected automatically).

Want me to try that approach? It would be much more likely to work in a Docker container.

Looking at the log, the key error is "Another compositing manager is running on screen 0". This suggests that maybe xrdp sesman has started an X server BEFORE the xfce4 session that is also trying to use compositing.
